# Deviations register — raw inventory (lane 28a)

**VERDICTS COME LATER (28b).** This file is the mechanical inventory only: every marker hit below is a raw row — no verdicts, no triage, no fixes. The category column is a provisional guess from the `28-deviations-audit.md` table (honest `unknown` where unsure); 28b assigns the real category + verdict.

## Sweep parameters

- **HEAD:** `01cdbd51aeb8a1ee78744e51c172618c6f810f41` (worktree `pi-worktree-d75260e1…s0-0`), tree clean at sweep time.
- **Swept:** `crates/`, `.rs` files only, excluding `tests/` and `target/`.
- **Markers (case-insensitive, in this order):** `not ported`, `unported`, `not implemented`, `documented no-op`, `no-op`, `placeholder`, `TODO(`, `FIXME`, `divergenc`, `approximat`, `simplif`, `out of scope`, `stub`, `the port's analogue`, `no-op sink`, `Noop`, `NoAudio`, `NoSteam`, `NoDiscord`, `NoJoystick`.
- **Reproducible command (per marker, in the order above):**
  ```sh
  grep -rniF --include='*.rs' '<marker>' crates/ | grep -vE '(^|/)(tests|target)/'
  ```
  Dedup rule: a line matching several markers is recorded **once**, under the **first** marker in the list above (e.g. a `documented no-op` line is not also a `no-op` row). 260 unique hit lines (raw per-marker counts: 280).
- **Row order:** grouped by crate (alphabetical); within a crate, file path (byte order) then line number — the sweep's reproducible order.
- **C++ citation verification (D-014):** every `C++:` citation below was grepped against the C++ tree (`src/`, `include/`, `public/`) at the HEAD above. `extlibs/SSVStart` (and the other `extlibs/*` submodules) are **not checked out** at this HEAD (`git submodule status`: all `-`/empty), so citations into SSVStart headers (`GameEngine.hpp`, `GameWindow.hpp`, `GameTimer.hpp`, `GameState.hpp`, `Manager.hpp`, `TimerBase`, `msUpdate`, `runEvents`, `GameEngine`, `ssvs::TimerStatic`, `ssvs::getGlobalBottom`, `LuaScripting::doc`) are marked **UNVERIFIABLE** — neither confirmed nor refuted; 28b should check out `extlibs/SSVStart` before ruling on them.
- **No row was invented:** every row is a real marker hit; `tests/` is excluded (test doubles are not deviations) but doc-comment module headers are included.

| F4 | `crates/oh-render/src/gl.rs` (`GlParts::set_swap_interval`) + `crates/oh-render/src/window.rs` (`Window::set_vsync` live-apply, `ensure_android_gl`'s apply-at-GL-materialization) + `crates/oh-app/src/windowed.rs` (`tick_fields` re-applying the config vsync — the C++ `mustRecreate` slot) | (promoted from K1.) The port never requested an EGL swap interval, and `Window::set_vsync` only stored the bool. The C++ applies `renderWindow.setVerticalSyncEnabled(vsync)` in `recreateWindow` after every window recreation, so its swap interval is always an explicit `eglSwapInterval(display, vsync ? 1 : 0)`; The port's winit/GLES surface never called `eglSwapInterval`, leaving the driver default in place (interval 0 / non-blocking on this Adreno 610 driver — the pre-fix menu free-ran at ~500 fps). | C++: `extlibs/SSVStart/include/SSVStart/GameSystem/GameWindow.hpp:94` (`recreateWindow`'s `setVerticalSyncEnabled(vsync)`; the setter's `mustRecreate` flag :192-195), `src/SSVOpenHexagon/Core/main.cpp:308-310` (`window->setVsync(hg::Config::getVsync())` at startup); pinned SFML fork `2f11710`: `src/SFML/Window/Window.cpp:274-280` (the runtime apply while the context is active — the observable behavior), `:113` (an Android window's context is the `EglContext`), `src/SFML/Window/EglContext.cpp:197-200` (`eglSwapInterval(m_display, enabled ? 1 : 0)`) — verified | **025j** (commits `1dde9ef9`, `5a34cffb`, `dc02da8f`): `GlParts::set_swap_interval` via glutin 0.32's `GlSurface::set_swap_interval` — EGL backend → `eglSwapInterval(display, 1 \| 0)` (glutin 0.32.3 `src/api/egl/surface.rs:369-378`, the same display-level call SFML makes; the GLX/WGL/CGL backends get the platform equivalent); `Window::set_vsync` applies it live on the live GL (SFML's observable behavior — the C++'s full window re-creation is the documented port gap, lane 029w); `ensure_android_gl` applies the stored vsync at GL materialization (the C++ `recreateWindow` always sets it explicitly after context creation, so the port must not rely on the driver default — which is interval 0 here — making the setting authoritative); the options-menu toggle (config write) re-applies on the next `tick_fields` (the C++ `mustRecreate` re-apply slot). On-device (Adreno 610, 1600×720, shipped `_RELEASE/config.json` vsync=false/limit_fps=true/max_fps=200, live-toggled through the port's own options menu): in-game vsync OFF — period 2.4–2.9 ms (~360 fps on the light tutorial level), gl.clear 0.4–0.5 ms, msDraw 1.7–3.4 ms, process CPU 76% of one core; in-game vsync ON — period 16.6–17.7 ms (60 fps vblank-locked), gl.clear 5–11 ms (GPU drain at frame start — the 025i ~10.9 ms back-pressure signature), msDraw 4.9–8.1 ms, CPU 47%; menu OFF ~500 fps/71% → ON 60 fps/45%. The OFF numbers match the C++'s explicit `eglSwapInterval(0)` semantics (an uncapped, CPU-hungry loop by design); the live toggle flips the behavior both ways on-device (2.5 ms ↔ 16.7 ms period). Host gate 750 tests (749 + `set_vsync_round_trips_and_applies_on_a_live_context`). See also K2 (the 200 fps cap is not enforced on Android — separate pre-existing deviation, unfixed) — 042: **CLOSED — HOLDS** (re-verified 2026-10-18 at HEAD: `GlParts::set_swap_interval` at gl.rs:900 + the `Window::set_vsync` live-apply; 025j landed) |

| K2 | `crates/oh-render/src/window.rs` (`RunHandler::about_to_wait`'s `WaitUntil` pacing + 025i's per-frame `request_redraw`) | the port's frame-rate cap (`effective_framerate_limit()` → `ControlFlow::WaitUntil(now + 1/limit)` — the C++ `setFramerateLimit` sleep) is NOT enforced on Android: winit 0.30's android `poll_events_with_timeout` short-circuits to a zero wait whenever a redraw is pending (winit 0.30.13 `src/platform_impl/android/mod.rs:549-554`), and 025i's ANR fix re-requests a redraw after EVERY frame, so `pending_redraw` is always true → the deadline never blocks → the loop free-runs. Measured on-device 2026-10-16 (shipped `limit_fps=true, max_fps=200`): menu ~500 fps, in-game ~360 fps on the light tutorial level, where the C++'s `Window::display` paces every sub-5 ms iteration to 200 fps (`sleep(5 ms − elapsed)` after the swap; the clock restarts each paced display, so a ≥5 ms iteration sleeps 0). The divergence is mostly invisible in heavy levels (both are GPU-limited — the C++ ~46 fps ≈ the port's back-pressure-limited rate) but the menu / light levels run 2–3× faster than the C++'s cap (CPU 76–71% vs the C++'s paced lower burn). The id plumbing works end-to-end up to `RenderStates`: the level Lua's `set_fragment_shader(rs, id)` → `oh-lua/src/api/shaders.rs:400` → `status.fragment_shader_ids[rs]` (`oh-game/src/status.rs:74`) → the bridge's `FrameInput.fragment_shader_ids` → `render_states_for_stage` → `RenderStates::with_shader(id)` (`oh-render/src/graphics.rs:102-122`), which also fires the `on_render_stage` Lua hook (the C++'s `runLuaFunctionIfExists` half — pinned by `oh-app/tests/bridge.rs`). But EVERY `GlDrawTarget` draw path drops `states.shader` (`windowed.rs:1091-1126`: "the per-stage shader binding is the issue-10 GL surface (the batch uploads with the default program)" — the vertex-colour `GlBatchUploader` program has no UV/sampler outputs at all, so even a translated fragment shader could not sample from it), and the level Lua's uniform sets have nowhere to go (`compose.rs:134-150` no-ops). In the C++ the stage's `getRenderStates` returns `sf::RenderStates{assets.getShaderByShaderId(*fragmentShaderId)}` (HGGraphics.cpp:77) and SFML compiles/links/executes that shader for EVERY draw of the stage, its uniforms set by the level Lua through `shdr_setUniform*` → `shader.setUniformUnsafe` (LuaScripting.cpp:1346, :1377+) — the shipped levels `pointless.lua` (steamart.frag on 8 scene stages, uniforms set every `onUpdate`) and `text_shader.lua` (text.frag on the Text stage) depend on it. **Why a full implementation is more than a mechanical wire-up** (29n1's verification): (a) GLSL SOURCE loading — the port's `oh-assets` `LoadedShader` carries only `path`/`shader_type`/`id` (shaders.rs module doc: "GLSL compilation is issue 10's job"); (b) desktop-GLSL → GLSL-ES-100 translation beyond the existing `translate_for_gles` (`oh-render/src/shader.rs:212` — the shipped `text.frag` uses `gl_TexCoord[0]` / `texture()`, mapping/rendering-menu.md:3672); (c) an SFML-style fixed-function VERTEX shader (position/`gl_Color`/UV) for every primitive batch — the current batch program carries no texture coordinates; (d) per-shader GL program objects (compile/link + SFML's fail-soft: a failed load logs and the stage renders shaderless); (e) UNIFORM STORAGE — the `shdr_setUniform*` values have nowhere to live today (the compose.rs no-ops; `oh-assets` keeps no uniforms; `oh_render::shader::Uniform` exists but is unused) and must be uploaded per draw, per program, per frame; (f) re-routing EVERY `RenderStates`-carrying draw path (the vertex batches, the text, the rectangles, the sprites) to select the bound program; (g) preserving the C++'s headless semantics (LuaScripting.cpp:1354-1360: `checkValidRenderStage` → headless → the `shdr_*` API is a no-op). This is the "issue-10 GL surface" the `oh-assets` / `compose` / `windowed` docs explicitly defer — a new subsystem, not a wire-up | C++: HGGraphics.cpp:74-77 (the `getRenderStates` shader binding + the `onRenderStage` hook), Assets.cpp:1139/1717 (`getShaderByShaderId`), LuaScripting.cpp:1346/1377+ (`withValidShaderId` + the `shdr_setUniform*` → `setUniformUnsafe`), LuaScripting.cpp:1354-1360 (the headless no-op gate) — verified; port: draw.rs:83-90 (the `shader: Option<usize>` carrier), graphics.rs:102-122 (the binding + the hook), windowed.rs:1091-1126 (the drop), shaders.rs:14 ("GLSL compilation is issue 10's job"), compose.rs:134-150 (the uniform no-ops) — verified | **FILED** (029e's 29n1, 2026-10-17 — verified, not implemented, per the task's "stop and record the precise gap" instruction): no fix lane filed yet [SUPERSEDED — see the closure below]. Until then, custom levels' shader effects are INVISIBLE in the windowed build **CLOSED (029n1/K6, 2026-10-17, landing `4c86855d` — "the pack fragment shaders EXECUTE on GL", issue 29 complete):** the execution half landed — the per-stage pack `.frag` programs are compiled (the C++-shaped fragment-only desktop program, on the F30 compatibility-profile context), bound by `draw_vertices`, with the draw-time `onRenderStage(stage, 60/fps)` hook firing BEFORE each stage's draw and a compile guard over all four shipped `.frag` files. The spec's own verification columns ran, and their negative control caught a REAL bug (a `gl.use_program`-ordering error). Verified here too: `cargo test -p oh-app --test pack_shaders` = 9/9, and the `pointless` level ON-vs-OFF differs by 7359/7500 pixels. The plumbing this row names (`draw.rs`'s `RenderStates.shader`, `graphics.rs`'s `render_states_for_stage`) is unchanged — the GL side now consumes it.** (the id plumbing + the `on_render_stage` hook work, so level scripts that only read stages or set uniforms behave — only the visual effect is missing) — 042: **CLOSED — HOLDS** (029n1, `4c86855d` — re-verified 2026-10-18 at HEAD: `cargo test -p oh-app --test pack_shaders` = 9/9 pass, and the GL half ran under `xvfb-run` (`cargo run -p oh-app --example pack_shaders_gl`): §4.2 all four shipped `.frag` compile guards + the `gles` child column, §4.3a `gradient.frag` executed by the shader-bound draw (the pixel readback shows the expected red/blue gradient vs the white default-program control), §4.3b `pointless` ON-vs-OFF = **7359/7500** pixels differ — the exact 029n1 number) |
| K7 | the draw-path Lua hook (`onRenderStage`, HGGraphics.cpp:73-74) executing Lua from the RENDER path | the C++ has ONE global `pcg32` for gameplay AND cosmetics; the port **splits** `cosmetic_rng`. A level whose `onRenderStage` draws random numbers therefore consumes the port's COSMETIC stream where the C++ consumes the GAMEPLAY stream — a latent gameplay divergence for third-party content. The shipped corpus does not hit it (`pointless.lua`'s onRenderStage only sets uniforms), which is consistent with 030c's 3627/3627 identical frames. Found by 029n1/K6 and flagged for the 030b oracle's owner; NOT fixed. — 042: **STILL TRUE** (re-verified 2026-10-18: the cosmetic/gameplay RNG split is still live — `hexagon_game.rs:433` `cosmetic_rng: Arc<dyn CosmeticRng>`; nothing since the triage has unified it) |

## Report — 029j batch (the joystick subsystem + the bind-name/hint surface: 29j1, 29a3, 29a2 — one commit each)

**29j1** (uservisible-gameplay, this commit) — the joystick's DOUBLE no-op is closed. (1) **The poller**: new `crates/oh-app/src/joystick.rs` — `RealJoystick` (desktop-cfg-gated: `gilrs` moved to the `not(any(target_os = "android", target_family = "wasm"))` target table in `oh-app/Cargo.toml`; Android/wasm keep `NoJoystick`/`NoMenuJoystick` — the C++'s `isConnected(0) == false` state, documented in the module docs): reads the FIRST connected gamepad (the C++'s hardcoded `joyId = 0`, Joystick.cpp:131) via `GilrsBuilder::with_default_filters(false)` (raw values — the C++ applies its ONE `Config::getJoystickDeadzone()` deadzone in the machine, Joystick.cpp:111-127; gilrs's default deadzone/jitter/dpad-to-button filters would double-filter) + `with_force_feedback(false)` (the C++ never uses FF), snapshots it into the SFML-scale `oh_config::JoystickPoll` (sticks `value × 100` with Y inverted to SFML's down-positive — Joystick.cpp:150-154 + the machine's `y_is` negate; the hat buttons → POV ±127 — the Joystick.cpp:30-36 dpad mapping; the 8 SFML `isButtonPressed` slots = the XBOX 360 positions per the Joystick.cpp:12-30 mapping comment, read through the mapping database's `South/East/North/West/LeftTrigger2/RightTrigger2/Select/Start`), and feeds the shared `oh_config::joystick` machine. ONE poller behind BOTH seams: the engine's `Arc<dyn Joystick>` and the new menu `Rc<dyn MenuJoystick>` (`RealMenuJoystick`, an `Arc`-backed clone — `MenuJoystick::update/pressed_focus/rising_edge/rising_edge_dir/ignore_all_presses`) are two views of the same `RealJoystick` (the C++'s one global; the machine it feeds is global too). `Gilrs` is `!Send + !Sync` → documented `unsafe impl Send + Sync` (both seams are driven only on the composition's main thread — the `SharedLua` raw-pointer precedent, `compose.rs` module docs). A failed backend open degrades to the no-controller state (logged once; the C++ SFML silent-degrades — the stderr line is the documented divergence). **System requirements (reported per the task, not silently added): Linux BUILD needs the `libudev` system library** (the transitive `libudev-sys`, a Linux-only dep of `gilrs-core` — verified in its Cargo.toml target table; `libudev-dev` at build time; `.github/workflows/rust.yml` now carries the portability note + a defensive `pkg-config --exists libudev || apt-get install -y libudev-dev` step on the Linux gate job — Windows gate/cross-check and Android/wasm builds are untouched), and the Linux RUNTIME needs hidraw access (non-root users need a udev rule granting `/dev/hidraw*` — gilrs ships `60-gilrs.rules`). (2) **The binds**: the `HexagonGame::new` ctor now calls `config.load_all_joystick_binds()` (the C++ ctor's `Config::loadAllJoystickBinds()`, HexagonGame.cpp:445-448 — read lock, after the input-registration block, the C++ order), and the windowed `refreshBinds` slot now runs the C++ `loadAllJoystickBinds()` tail (MenuGame.cpp:3373-3388 @3387) after the keyboard trigger refresh loop, so a rebind re-feeds the machine (the `ConfigMenuAdapter::refresh_binds` comment updated accordingly). The engine's `updateInput_UpdateJoystickControls` (HGUpdate.cpp:560-586 — window-gated, the poll, the four `setInputImplIfFalse` held inputs, the Exit/ForceRestart∥Restart/Replay `risingEdge` arms) was ported by the previous lane and now reads the live machine (its doc updated); the `Joystick` seam gained `vendor_id()` (the C++ `getJoystickBindName`'s `isConnected(0) ? getIdentification(0).vendorId : 0` vendor source, Config.cpp:1577-1581 — the input 29a2's hint block needs). The poller's snapshot is the thin untested wrapper the task anticipated; the poll→machine→handler contract it implements is exactly the surface the three test files pin.

**29a3** (internal, this commit) — the C++ game-ctor's second config side effect is now live at the ctor point, in the C++ order: `HexagonGame::new` runs `config.keyboard_binds_sanity_check()` (the C++ `Config::keyboardBindsSanityCheck()`, HexagonGame.cpp:345) and THEN `config.load_all_joystick_binds()` (the C++ `Config::loadAllJoystickBinds()`, HexagonGame.cpp:448 — landed in 29j1). Verified from source: `Config.cpp:1392-1406` (the 12-trigger scope: `triggerRotateCCW()..triggerDown()` — NextPack/PreviousPack/LuaConsole/Pause NOT in scope) and `Config.cpp:1363-1390` (`resizeTrigger`: drop `isUnbound()` combos, `pop_back` while `> maxBinds`, `emplace_back(Combo({KKey::Unknown}))` while `< maxBinds`) — the port's `resize_trigger` + `keyboard_binds_sanity_check` were already the verbatim port (only the call site was missing; `MAX_BINDS = 4` = the C++ `maxBinds`), so the fix is the call, not a re-implementation. The repair semantics are REPAIR, not validate: a malformed `config.json` trigger (5 combos with scattered unbound slots) comes out of the ctor as 4 combos, the bound ones agglomerated at the front, the tail cleared — on the shared `Arc<RwLock<Config>>` the menu reads through (the C++'s one global `Config`). Tests: `oh-config/tests/bind_sanity_check.rs` (5: `resize_trigger_agglomerates_trims_and_pads`, `resize_trigger_trims_overflow_from_the_tail` (the 5th bound combo is the one dropped — the C++ `pop_back`), `resize_trigger_pads_short_triggers`, `keyboard_binds_sanity_check_repairs_exactly_the_cpp_twelve` (all 16 triggers malformed; the 12 repaired, the other 4 verbatim), `keyboard_binds_sanity_check_is_idempotent_after_the_first_run`) + `oh-game/tests/bind_ctor_effects.rs` (1: `game_ctor_runs_the_cpp_keyboard_sanity_and_joystick_bind_load` — the ctor-level proof for BOTH side effects on one deliberately malformed config: the 5-combo `t_restart` repaired on the shared config + the custom `j_restart`/`j_replay` slots reaching the machine (the discriminator: button 0 — the DEFAULT j_restart slot — reads as unbound after the ctor, where the un-repaired-defaults machine would read it as `Jid::Restart`)). Not established: nothing host-relevant (pure data-layer + ctor wiring; the C++ behavior is pinned citation-for-citation).

**29a2** (uservisible-cosmetic, this commit) — the C++ `newGame`'s restart/replay input-hint block (HexagonGame.cpp:758-821) is now in `new_game` VERBATIM, under the `self.0.window` gate (the C++ `window != nullptr`): `get_keyboard_bind_names(Tid::Restart/Replay)` (the first key only — the `find(',')` + `truncate` trim, the C++'s `commaPos > 0` gate) → `get_joystick_bind_name(Jid::Restart/Replay, self.0.joystick.vendor_id())` (the vendor arrives through the `Joystick` seam's `vendor_id()` — 29j1's seam method; `NoJoystick` = 0 = the C++'s `isConnected(0) == false` read, `RealJoystick` = the connected gamepad's vendor) → the three-branch assembly with the C++'s exact literals and trailing `\n` in every form ("PRESS <keys> TO RESTART", " OR JOYSTICK <btn>", "PRESS JOYSTICK <btn> TO RESTART", "NO RESTART BUTTON SET" + the replay twins). The `update_text` read side (the `has_died` gate, update.rs:1415-1418) was already ported, so the strings now actually reach the on-screen text. Verified from source: HexagonGame.cpp:758-821 (the block), Config.cpp:1455-1510 (`getKeyboardBindNames`), Config.cpp:1568-1603 (`getJoystickBindName` — the value-33 → "" @1578-1580, the vendor read @1586-1589, MS 0x045E / Sony 0x54C columns with the `value >= 12` guard, the plain-number fallback @1603). Test: `oh-game/tests/restart_replay_hints.rs` (1 test, scenarios A–G, character-for-character string assertions): the vendor-0 defaults (the plain-number fallback: "PRESS SPACE OR JOYSTICK 0 TO RESTART\n"), the MS 0x045E names ("A" / "BACK"), the Sony 0x54C names ("SQUARE" / "L2"), the joystick-only variant (cleared t_restart → "PRESS JOYSTICK SQUARE TO RESTART\n"), the nothing-set variants ("NO RESTART BUTTON SET\n" / "NO REPLAY BUTTON SET\n" — j_* = 33), the `value >= 12` guard (the keyboard-only form with no " OR JOYSTICK" half), and the headless composition (window = false → both strings empty, the C++ gate). `compose_with_config` was added to `tests/common/mod.rs` (`compose_with_renderer` now delegates to it) so the tests can inject specific `t_*`/`j_*` bind values. ## Report — 029b batch (the four menu input/navigation fixes: 29b2, 29b5, 29b7, 29b8 — one commit each)

**29b2** (uservisible-gameplay, commit `ea82d441`) — the dropped mouse wheel is plumbed end-to-end and the C++ handler (MenuGame.cpp:299-356) ported. The `MouseWheelScrolled` fold arm (previously `{ .. } => {}`) now records the delta into a new per-window `wheel_events_sink` (one entry per event, arrival order — the C++ `runEvents` per-event order the drain preserves); `MenuSlotAdapter::refresh_input` drains it per recorded delta and feeds each to the new `MenuGame::handle_mouse_wheel` (the C++ handler 1:1 — `Shared.wheel_progress` = the C++ `wheelProgress{0.f}`: SMain/other menus accumulate to a past-±1 line then fire `up_action` / `down_action` and reset; LevelSelection `focusHeld` → `change_pack_quick(delta > 0 ? -1 : 1)` (the C++ `upAction` focus-held arm), else `y_scroll_to += delta * 48.0` with the `> 0 → 0` top clamp and the `< -4000` end-scroll → `a35_eagerformore` unlock (the new `UnlockAchievementCallback` seam — the production composition wires the real `steam.unlock_achievement`) + `error.ogg` + `y_scroll_to = 0`). **The wheel-inversion trap was checked from source, not assumed**: the pinned SFML fork's `Event.hpp` documents `mouseWheelScroll.delta` as "positive is up/left", and winit 0.30.13's X11 backend maps wheel-up (Button4) → `LineDelta(0, +1)` / wheel-down (Button5) → `LineDelta(0, -1)` (the Windows `WM_MOUSEWHEEL` arm is positive-on-up too) — the conventions MATCH, so the port applies the winit delta to the C++'s expressions with NO sign flip; `map_window_event`'s `PixelDelta` half scales `pos.y / 10.0` (the established touch scale). Citations re-verified: MenuGame.cpp:299-356 (incl. the :319-321 `focusHeld` ternary, :324-331 the `* 48.f` + top clamp, :332-339 the end-scroll, :344-354 the `wheelProgress` arms + reset), MenuGame.cpp:187 (`wheelProgress{0.f}`), SFML fork `Event.hpp` (the delta sign), winit 0.30.13 `event_processor.rs` (the Button4/Button5 + `WM_MOUSEWHEEL` mappings). Tests: `oh-menu/tests/wheel.rs` (8 — synthetic deltas incl. both clamp ends: the strict `> 1.f` / `< -1.f` boundaries, the wrap-from-index-0 up arm, the `* 48.f` accumulation, the top clamp, the end-scroll at exactly the bound (the `-4000` non-fire) and past it (the `a35_eagerformore` + `error.ogg` + reset-to-0), the per-event clamp order, the `focusHeld` quick pack change incl. the zero-delta arm); `oh-render` window tests (2 — the fold's per-event recording + the no-sink no-op; the winit `LineDelta` / `PixelDelta` → SFML-sign mapping); `oh-app` `wheel_chain_drives_the_menu_navigation` (1 — the full raw-event chain: `map_window_event` → fold → sink → `refresh_input` drain → handler, the wheel-UP lines firing `up_action` across frames and the wheel-DOWN wrap). All 16 `MenuGame::new` call sites updated to the new `unlock_achievement` seam arg (the 29b8d `ELoginSuccess` unlock reuses the same seam). NOT established: the on-device wheel feel (the phone's ADB listener is down for this batch — no device work per the hygiene rule; the host tests carry the correctness argument, the device visual is PENDING). Gate green: 828 tests (817 + 11).

**29b5** (uservisible-gameplay, commit `1d277054`) — the level-list scroll machinery (audit row 5 + the D10 `calcScrollSpeed`/list-height math) is ported 1:1. The pure float math now lives in `oh-menu` as a `Shared` method cluster + `layout::LevelSelectionLayout` / `layout::EPSILON` (the C++ `updateLevelSelectionDrawingParameters` formula as a pure function of the selection font's probed heights — the same values `oh-app`'s `menu_draw` refresh caches for the draw side, so the scroll math and the draw side run on identical constants): (1) `PackChange::Rest` now runs `scroll_level_list_to_target_y` (the C++ :4578-4598 easing: `YOffset` approaches `YScrollTo` at `scrollSpeed` units/s, clamped to land EXACTLY on the target, the `Utils::epsilon` (1e-4) arrival gate) — `y_scroll_to` is finally CONSUMED (previously written by `change_pack_action`'s stop-scroll and read nowhere); (2) `up_action` / `down_action` carry the C++ level-change arms in the C++ order (`setIndex` FIRST, then `calc_level_change_scroll(±2)` — the favorites pack-0 pretense, the `currentIndex < 2` top branch, the `size − 2` bottom branch whose next-pack-label target goes NEGATIVE for lists taller than the window), plus the single-pack top wrap (`setIndex(size − 1)` + `calcScrollSpeed` + the `scroll > h − YOffset` → `YScrollTo = h − scroll` test) and bottom wrap (`setIndex(0)` + `calcScrollSpeed` + `YScrollTo = 0`); (3) `calc_scroll_speed` now scales with the level count (`30 × max(count / 16, 1)` — the D10 half; the level-change path keeps the C++'s base-speed reset at :4406); (4) `change_pack_quick` carries the C++ :2156-2181 compensation (the previous pack label's top prevails — the `YScrollTo = YOffset = target` drawer jump, no easing — else the next pack label's bottom with the `min(packIdx + 2, packCount)` clamp + `levelLabelHeight + 3 × slctFrameSize`); (5) `calc_pack_change_scroll_fold` / `calc_pack_change_scroll_stretch` ported verbatim (the `−2` early returns, the `specialAction` short-cut's `scrollTop < −YOffset` cut for long lists, the last-pack branch, the "the bottom must prevail" `if(!WithResult)` tails) and called PER FRAME from the `Folding` / `Stretching` arms (the `Folding` arm's missing `changePack` → `packChangeOffset = getLevelListHeight()` + `calcScrollSpeed` sequence restored — pre-fix the fold switched the pack but never re-armed the scroll); (6) the focus rising/falling edge arms (`update`, the C++ :2645-2675 block) now run `quick_pack_fold_stretch` (the collapsed-row re-layout + `adjustLevelsOffset` — pre-fix documented no-ops); (7) `get_level_list_height` now the C++ formula (`levelLabelHeight × (focusHeld ? 1 : size) + slctFrameSize` — pre-fix a coarse `30.0 × count`), and `get_level_selection_height` ported for API parity (NO C++ call site — declared + defined but never invoked in MenuGame.cpp; recorded, not used). Citations re-verified from source: MenuGame.cpp:2882-2934, :4298-4312, :4388-4395, :4397-4402, :4404-4461, :4463-4479, :4481-4561, :4563-4576, :4578-4598, :4659-4711, :1964-1981, :1990-1999, :2056-2070, :2079-2086, :2156-2181, :2645-2675; Utils/Geometry.hpp:11; MenuGame.hpp:422-423. **29b7** (uservisible-gameplay, commit `21ce8551`) — the hold-Exit-to-quit is live. The C++ `update()` tail's `if(exitTimer > 20) window.stop();` (MenuGame.cpp:2852-2854) had no port: `add_exit_timer` (the hardcoded Escape `Type::Always` bind's on-half, MenuGame.cpp:965-971 — `if(state != MOpts) exitTimer += mFT`) and `reset_exit_timer` (the off-half's `exitTimer = 0`) were already ported, but nothing consumed `exit_timer` — holding Exit/Escape for 20 seconds did nothing. The fix is the `update()` tail check through the EXISTING request path: `if (exit_timer > 20.0) { quit(); }` (`quit()` → `quit_requested` → the windowed loop's break condition — the port's `window.stop()` equivalent; the strict `>` preserved, exactly 20.0 does not quit), placed at the port's `update()` tail (the rotation-accumulator block — the C++'s tail position after the touch-grid block). Both stale comments that claimed the consumer was "the unported `MenuGame::draw` lane (issue 13b)" are rewritten — WRONG: the C++ check is in `update()`, not `draw()`, and is fully portable. Citations re-verified: MenuGame.cpp:2852-2854, :963-971 (the `{{k::Escape}}` `t::Always` bind — on-half's MOpts gate + off-half's reset). Tests: `oh-menu/tests/exit_hold.rs` (3 — the threshold boundary (20.0 exactly = no quit, the 21st frame fires), the release reset (two 19 s holds with a reset between never quit), the MOpts no-accumulation gate). NOT established: the on-device 20s-hold (no device work this batch — the phone's ADB listener is down; the threshold + reset + gate are pinned host-side, the device confirmation is PENDING). Gate green: 843 tests (840 + 3).


**29b8** (uservisible-cosmetic, this commit) — the four cosmetic/input gaps ported. (a) **`randomTip` populated**: the C++ ctor's `randomTip = tips[ssvu::getRndI(0, tips.size())]` (MenuGame.cpp:752-766, the four verbatim two-line tip pairs) now runs through `oh_rng`'s `Pcg32Fast` (the oracle-pinned `pcg32_fast`) in a thread-local engine seeded with the same entropy convention as oh-game's `initialize_rng` (SystemTime nanos ^ pid rotated 32 — the C++'s `std::random_device`-seeded global; both live/non-deterministic); `getRndI`'s inclusive-`uniform_int_distribution` semantics = `uniform_int(rng, 0, 3)`. (b) **credits-bar 3-texture cycle**: `Shared.current_credits_id` accumulates per frame at the `update()` tail (the C++ `currentCreditsId += mFT`, MenuGame.cpp:2848) and `credits_bar2_id()` computes `(id / 100.0) as i32`'s `rem_euclid(3)` — the C++ `getByModIdx(creditsIds, toInt(currentCreditsId / 100))` (hpp:135-137 the three texture names; Math.hpp:292 `getMod` = the euclidean modulo). The `menu_draw` snapshot carries the id + the draw-site comment: the VISIBLE cycle awaits the 15c texture table (all three textures are the same 1×1 placeholder today — state-only for now). (c) **Shift modifier**: the C++ `leftRightActionImpl` modifier's `isKeyPressed(LShift) || isKeyPressed(RShift)` half (MenuGame.cpp:1860-1862) now ORs into the port's modifier via the new `Shared.shift_held` (the `MenuGame::set_shift_held` hook — the menu has no window access in oh-menu; the app layer's `MenuSlotAdapter::refresh_input` feeds it per frame from the `InputState` LShift/RShift bits — the C++'s live keyboard read), so shift+left/right steps the item's `increase`/`decrease` by 2, not 1 (the C++'s `step = 2` loop, :1863-1866). (d) **`a23_login` unlock on `ELoginSuccess`**: `handle_net_event`'s `ELoginSuccess` arm now calls `self.unlock_achievement("a23_login")` after the dialog box shows (MenuGame.cpp:2590-2593's lambda order — dialog, then unlock), reusing the 29b2 `UnlockAchievementCallback` seam (the production composition wires the real Steam unlock). Citations re-verified: MenuGame.cpp:752-766, :2848-2850, :1860-1866, :2590-2593; MenuGame.hpp:135-137; SSVUtils Rnd.hpp:21-22/:44-50, Math.hpp:292. ## Report — 029b-fav batch (the favorites drawer + the created-profile menu item: 29b1, 29b6 — one commit each)

**29b1** (uservisible-gameplay, commit `ae9b305e`) — the favorites feature, dead end-to-end, is live over the 029c1 store. The 029c1 data layer (the profile store's favorite persistence, `is_level_favorite` over the real current profile, the `ProfileData` favorite mutations) had landed with ZERO consumers: `favorites_active` was never set true (the only writer a `menu_draw` test), `fav_slct.level_data_ids` was never built, F1 / F2 were `TODO` no-op stubs (the windowed binds `windowed.rs:1870-1893` + the `update()` joystick arms), and `change_favorite_levels_to_profile` was a stub deferring to "a 13b detail" — CHECKED: the 13b lane landed only the DRAW side (the favorites drawer's render + the `"FAVORITES"` pack label, `menu_draw.rs` — the C++ `MenuGame.cpp:5199-5201`'s `tempString = "FAVORITES"`; the task's `"FAVORITES:"` spelling does not exist in the C++), never the state machine. The C++ state machine is now ported 1:1: (a) `change_favorite_levels_to_profile` (MenuGame.cpp:4781-4833) rebuilds the list from the profile's favorites (the new `MenuAssets::favorite_level_ids` → the new `oh_assets::Assets::get_favorite_level_ids` on the current profile; no current profile = the C++ `getCurrentLocalProfile()` assert's port no-op arm, as `is_level_favorite`'s), sorted by the lowercased level name (`ssvu::toLower` = per-char `std::tolower` → `to_lowercase` — NOT the id order), the no-favorites arm forcing `favorites_active = false` (the C++ `lvlDrawer = &lvlSlct`), the index clamp, the `lvl_offsets.resize(sz, 0.0)` (the C++ `resize`'s keep-existing + zero-tail); (b) `Shared::add_remove_favorite_level` (MenuGame.cpp:4835-4931) ports the add arm (the store insert via the new `MenuAssets::add_favorite_level` → `Assets::add_favorite_level`, the `lvl_offsets` push, `is_level_favorite = true`, the lowercased-name sorted insert, the "just in case" clamp) and the remove arm (the store erase, the list `retain` + `lvl_offsets.pop()` — the C++'s `pop_back` quirk preserved — the index clamp/reset, the conditional `adjust_levels_offset`, the empty-list drawer exit (`fav_slct.x_offset = 0` + `favorites_active = false`), the no-empty-space scroll test, the `set_index` looks-update on the possibly-switched drawer — which re-derives `is_level_favorite` exactly as the C++ `setIndex` does, favorites arm `= true` / regular arm profile re-read) and the trailing `select.ogg`; (c) `Shared::switch_to_from_favorite_levels` (MenuGame.cpp:4933-4955) — the guard (the `state != LevelSelection || favSlct.levelDataIds->empty()` no-op), the in-flight-fold `change_pack()` completion, the outgoing drawer's `y_scroll_to = y_offset`, the `favorites_active` flip, the NEW drawer's `set_index` + `adjust_levels_offset` + `select.ogg`; (d) the F1 / F2 windowed binds + the `update()` joystick `Jid::AddToFavorites` / `Jid::FavoritesMenu` rising edges (the C++'s TWO separate `if`s — both may fire in one frame) now call the new `MenuGame::add_remove_favorite_level` / `MenuGame::switch_to_from_favorite_levels` wrappers; (e) the two LATENT favorites-conditional halves the rebuild activates (the audit's D1 + D7): `play_selected_level` now reads the ACTIVE drawer (the pack idx + the level id + the `get_nth_diff_mult` lookup on that level — the C++ `playSelectedLevel`'s `lvlDrawer` expressions, MenuGame.cpp:2369-2383; previously hardcoded `lvl_slct` — the wrong level once favorites were reachable) and `get_selectable_pack_infos_size` returns 1 in the favorites view (the C++ :4981-4983 arm — the `up_action` / `down_action` single-pack wrap arms + `change_pack_action`'s guard now run the C++ favorites behavior); (f) the `FakeAssets`'s `favorites` went flat-set → per-profile `BTreeMap` (the C++'s "each profile has its own favorite levels" — the flat set had mirrored ONE list across all profiles). Deviations: (1) the C++ remove arm's `favoriteLevelDataIds.erase(std::find(...))` is UB when the id is absent (it cannot be — the current level IS in the active list); the port's `retain` is the safe equivalent. **29b6** (uservisible-gameplay, this commit) — a created profile now APPEARS in the "profile selection" menu, at the C++'s sorted position. The C++ `okAction` (MenuGame.cpp:2211-2256) pushes the item AT THE SORTED POSITION: `profiles.create<ssvms::Items::Single>(enteredStr, [this, name = enteredStr]{ assets.pSetCurrent(name); changeFavoriteLevelsToProfile(); })` (the label is the BARE name — no prefix) + `profiles.sortByName()` (Category.hpp:58-65 — `a->getName() < b->getName()`, a case-sensitive byte compare — the port's `Category::sort_by_name` is the same comparator), and the port's `create_profile_from_entered` (the 029c1 data half) had created the profile + set it current + rebuilt the favorites list but never touched `profile_selection_menu` — a freshly created profile was unselectable until a restart (the boot-time `build_menus` repopulated the category from the store). The audit's question — is the port's `if entered_str.is_empty()` guard faithful? YES: the C++ wraps the whole creation block in `if(!enteredStr.empty())` (MenuGame.cpp:2214) — an empty name skips the `pCreate` call too — pinned by `ok_action_empty_entered_str_is_the_cpp_skip_arm`. The fix: `create_profile_from_entered` now returns `ProfileCreateResult` (`Skipped` / `Rejected` / `Created(name)` — the C++'s three exits named for their tail behaviour), and the new `MenuGame::add_profile_selection_item(name)` mirrors the C++'s `profiles.create<Single>` (the bare-name label, the `pSetCurrent` + `changeFavoriteLevelsToProfile` callback, the `sort_by_name()` re-sort) at the same call point (after `pCreate` / `pSetCurrent` / the rebuild, before the state change). `ok_action`'s arm now branches on the result and fixes three tail divergences found while restructuring: (1) the `ETLPNew` creation arm previously early-RETURNed like the boot arm — the C++ `break`s, so the select.ogg tail plays (now faithful); (2) the `ETLPNewBoot` arm's openHexagon.ogg + SMain + `return` (no select.ogg) preserved; (3) the duplicate-name guard previously `break`ed to the select.ogg tail — the C++ `return`s out of `okAction` WITHOUT it (now faithful). Deviation note: the C++'s duplicate check iterates the category's ITEM names; the port checks `assets.profile_names()` — equivalent in practice (the category is built from + kept in sync with the store by the same two call sites — the boot-time `build_menus` and this item add) and the store is the source of truth. Citations re-verified: MenuGame.cpp:2211-2256 (the full block incl. the :2214 guard, the :2220-2233 duplicate `return`, the :2235-2237 `pCreate`/`pSetCurrent`/`changeFavoriteLevelsToProfile`, the :2241-2247 item + re-sort, the :2249-2252 boot `return`, the :2253-2254 `break`), Category.hpp:58-65, SSVMenuSystem/Items/Single.hpp (the single `exec` = the action). Tests: `oh-menu/tests/profiles.rs` +6 (create → the item exists AT THE SORTED POSITION among pre-existing profiles, the store + current + `enteredStr` clear + SMain + the ETLPNew select.ogg tail; the ordering matches the C++ byte comparator — "Zebra" sorts BEFORE "apple", NOT a lowercased/case-insensitive order; the created item is SELECTABLE — its callback switches the current profile AND rebuilds the favorites list (the 29b1 rebuild) WITHOUT switching the drawer (the C++ item callback's `changeFavoriteLevelsToProfile` forces the regular drawer only in its empty arm — the F2 switch is separate); the `ETLPNewBoot` arm's openHexagon.ogg WITHOUT the select.ogg tail; the empty-name guard's faithfulness; the duplicate guard's `error.ogg` + dialog + `ignore_inputs` 2 + no creation + no item + no state advance + no select.ogg). NOT established: the on-device new-profile flow (the ADB listener is down for this batch — no device work, no temporary `debuggable` flag; the host tests carry the correctness argument, the device visual is PENDING). ## Report — 029b-dialog batch (the dialog input state machine + the dialog geometry drift: 29b3, D1-D6 — one commit each)

**29b3** (uservisible-gameplay, commit 1) — the last "the user literally cannot do this" menu gap closed: the online login / registration / delete-account flows were unreachable because (a) typed chars never reached `dialogBox.getInput()` (the C++'s 2nd `TextEntered` handler, MenuGame.cpp:491-517, was absent — `input_mut()` had exactly one call site, the default-fill), (b) the `checkCloseDialogBox` sequence machine (MenuGame.cpp:384-489) was unwired — `DialogInputState` was only assigned at entry, `handle_release_edge` returned early for `key_to_close != Unknown` and took no key, and `try_register` / `try_login` / `try_delete_account` were unreachable trait no-ops, and (c) the Escape cancel (MenuGame.cpp:552-561) was absent. All three ported: `handle_text_entered` now runs BOTH C++ `TextEntered` halves (the `enteredChars` feed + the echo — printable 32..126 capped at 32 chars with beep.ogg, Backspace (unicode 8) pop with beep.ogg); `handle_release_edge` now carries the released key's identity (`Option<oh_config::Key>`, fed per-edge by the windowed drain — keys `Some(key)`, mouse / joystick / finger `None`) and dispatches the C++'s three dialog halves (the close-key-gated `--ignoreInputs`, the Escape cancel, the full `check_close_dialog_box` machine with the exact transitions / strings / capture-then-closeBox order, the mouse / joystick `keyToClose != Unknown → return` half); the window fold restores SFML's `TextEntered(8)` for Backspace (winit carries no `text` for the key). Citations re-verified against the C++ tree: MenuGame.cpp:384-489 (the whole lambda: :386-391 closeBox, :408-410 the gate, :412-416 Nothing, :419-427 RegUsername, :430-438 RegPassword, :440-459 RegConfirm + tryRegister incl. the "PASSWORD MISMATCH\n\nPRESS ANY KEY OR BUTTON TO CLOSE THIS MESSAGE\n" dialog + error.ogg + setIgnoreAllInputs(1)), :491-517 (the echo: :501-508 the 32..126 push + 32-cap + beep, :509-516 the Backspace pop), :265-272 (the 1st handler — both fire per event: a Backspace reaches BOTH), :519-565 (the Key handler: :544-550 the close-key-gated decrement, :552-561 the Escape cancel, :563 the `checkCloseDialogBox()` call), :616-643 / :673-700 (the mouse / joystick dialog halves), :3119-3121 (the "Needs to be two" comment), :1607-1655 (the online items' entry states), :6107-6161 (the exact nice-dialog concat). Tests: `oh-menu/tests/state_machine.rs` +10 over a recording `NetworkClient` fake (no server): typing appends + the 32-cap holds; Backspace pops; the password re-dialog is masked (the exact "REGISTRATION / PASSWORD" nice string + `input_box_password`); a mismatched confirm shows the verbatim "PASSWORD MISMATCH" dialog + error.ogg + counter 1 and does NOT call register; the happy registration calls `try_register` once with the typed values; login calls `try_login`; delete-account calls `try_delete_account`; Escape cancels mid-sequence (state `Nothing`, box cleared, counter 0, the sequence dead afterward); the pointer releases do not advance the sequence; a non-close key does not decrement an input box. Deviation recorded: the C++'s `mustUseMenuItem` mouse-hover half (counter 1) rides the documented no-hover deviation (`menu_draw.rs:1593`) — the port holds counter 2 (the C++'s keyboard-navigation shape: the first Enter after the dialog opens is absorbed by the counter gate, exactly as in the C++).

**D1-D6** (uservisible-cosmetic, commit 2) — the dialog geometry drift ported: (D1) the `create` split no longer pushes the trailing segment (the C++'s exact shape, HexagonDialogBox.cpp:46-58 — every real dialog message ends in `\n`, so no call site loses its last line; `create("A")` → `empty() == true` as in the C++); (D2) the counter-2 close-timing landed with 29b3's counter gate (the port closed a `setIgnoreAllInputs(2)` box one release too early — now two, per the :3119-3121 comment); (D5) the four `drawText` offsets carry ONE `doubleFrameSize` (HexagonDialogBox.cpp:185-186 / :231-232 / :259-261 — the baseline was 24 px low / 24 px right); (D6) the dialog pass centers in the CONFIG resolution (the new `MenuConfig::resolution_width/height` seam = the C++ `Config::getWidth()/getHeight()`, Config.cpp:1102-1111; the `ConfigMenuAdapter` delegates to `oh_config::Config::get_width/get_height` — identical on a desktop window == config × pixelMult, now C++-correct on Android / a resized window where the port used to center as 1733 wide instead of 1024). (D3) + (D4) DELIBERATE (marked, not ported): the glyph oracles stay placeholders (the audit's SEAMS S3 / the D-011 rule) — the C++'s measured `lineHeight` (the SFML probe, :34) and `dialogWidth` (`getGlobalWidth`, :36) are the `LinearFontHeight` (`char_size × 0.7`) / `60 × len` "coarse width probe (slice 13b)" seam; a real-font oracle change ripples across the whole menu layout's golden tests (out of scope). (D7-D9) outside this task's D1-D6 list — left as-is (D7's edge case, D8's equivalent vocabulary, D9's internal surface). Tests: `oh-menu/tests/render.rs` +1 (`dialog_box_create_drops_the_trailing_segment` — the split's three shapes + `create("A")`'s `empty()`; `dialog_box_center_frame_geometry` updated to the D1 line count + total height) + `oh-app/src/menu_draw.rs` +2 (`dialog_text_offset_carries_one_double_frame_size` — the Center y + the TopLeft x/y pins over the REAL repo font; `dialog_centers_in_the_config_resolution_not_the_layout` — the 1024×768 config vs the 800×450 window: the center-line lands at 512, not the layout-space 682.67).

NOT established: the on-device dialog visuals (typing / the masked password field / the mismatch box / the geometry on the phone) — the phone's ADB listener is DOWN for this batch (`adb connect 192.168.10.138:38721` refused, the host still pings it); no device work, no temporary `debuggable` flag; the host tests carry the correctness argument (the state machines + the string pins + the geometry math are pinned host-side; the dialog's visual confirmation is PENDING for the next device session). Also not established: the C++'s `mustUseMenuItem` mouse-hover exec-timing (counter 1) — the documented no-hover deviation stands. ## Report — 029i batch (the asset reload paths + the F3/F4 handler + the workshop cache: 29i1, 29b9, 29p1 — one commit each)

**29i1** (uservisible-cosmetic — the debug-only reload tool, this commit) — the C++ reload trio is now `oh-assets`: `Assets::reload_all_shaders` (Assets.cpp:1159-1172 — re-load every registered shader, log the C++'s failure line — the `ImplImpl` typo kept — and `continue`), `Assets::reload_pack` (Assets.cpp:1174-1278) and `Assets::reload_level` (Assets.cpp:1280-1441), plus `RealMenuAssets::reload_assets` over them (the `MenuAssets` seam's new `String`-returning method + the new `reload_all_shaders` method — the F3/F4 handler's two calls). (a) **The store is now reload-mutable behind `&self`**: the boot-once `level_datas` / `level_data_ids_by_pack` / `music_path_map` / `music_data_map` / `style_data_map` / `storage` moved into a `Mutex<AssetState>` (the `Arc<Assets>` is shared menu↔engine, and the reload mutates through `&self` getters — the same short-lock pattern as the `profiles` store; `shaders` stays outside — the reload only READS it). Every affected getter now returns an owned value (`Option<LevelData>`, owned `Vec`/`HashMap` clones, owned `StyleData` / `MusicData`, owned paths) — the call sites in `oh-game` / `oh-audio` / `oh-app` / the test `common` modules updated accordingly. (b) **The C++'s exact result strings + early-return arms, mirrored**: the `reloadPack` section lines ("Levels successfully reloaded" … "Custom sound files successfully reloaded" / "invalid … folder path"), the `reloadLevel` per-file lines ("level data X.json successfully loaded", "style data …", "music data …", "no matching … found", "… is already loaded", "new custom sound file … successfully loaded"), the `invalid level folder path` early `return` (the whole function), the `nullSoundId` early return AFTER the music-file section, and the keying quirks the C++ carries (the `reloadPack` custom-sound buffer key = `packId + "_" + getFileName()` — WITH the `.ogg`; the `reloadLevel` sound key = `packId + "_" + soundId` — WITHOUT it; the `musicPathMap.emplace` first-wins on both paths; the deleted-file staleness — an erasure of a pack-level entry is NOT done, so a level deleted on disk keeps its stale `levelDatas` entry, exactly as the C++). Citations re-verified from source (Assets.cpp:1159-1172/:1174-1278/:1280-1441 incl. the :1265-1276 sound key, :1410-1441 the `reloadLevel` sound arms). (c) **The JSON-error class — the one recorded deviation**: the C++ reads through `ssvuj::getFromFile` (logs the read error, returns the Null object → the default-valued `LevelData` `id = "nullId"` in the level section; a field TYPE mismatch would abort the C++ uncaught in the `extr_*` chain). The port's `LevelData::from_json` / `MusicData::from_json` / `StyleData::from_json` return `Result` — on `Err` the port logs the C++'s `ssvuj::logReadError`-equivalent line and SKIPS the entry (the `reloadPack` level section: no `levelDatas` insert, no id push; the `reloadLevel` data sections: the error line, then the C++'s control flow — `reloadPack`'s `continue`, `reloadLevel`'s early `return`), instead of the C++'s abort. Same clean-error class as the boot-time `FatalLoadError` handling. Tests: `oh-assets/tests/reload.rs` (8 — THE ACCEPTANCE POINT `a_modified_level_file_is_seen_by_the_reload`: write `Packs/p/Levels/a.json` + `Styles/` + `Music/` (data + file) + a shader file, boot `Assets`, mutate the level JSON on disk (the `name` + `styleId` + `musicId` + `difficultyMults`), `reload_level` → the new values + the exact 4-line result string; the `reload_pack` full result string incl. the early `return` on a missing `Levels/`; the `nullId` default on an unparseable level file (the C++'s `getFromFile`-error + default-constructor shape); the deleted-file staleness (delete a level file → `level_datas` keeps the old entry, the result string still says loaded); the `reload_all_shaders` log line on a missing shader file + the registry invariance; the style/music data in-place replace + the `musicPathMap` first-wins; the `nullSoundId` early return with the music-file section already emitted). Gate green: 877 + 8 = 885.

**29b9** (uservisible-cosmetic — the debug-only F3/F4 reload, this commit) — the F3/F4 binds now run the C++'s `MenuGame::reloadAssets` handler (MenuGame.cpp:3092-3127) end-to-end. The binds themselves had existed since 010b (the windowed `register_menu_binds`' hardcoded F3/F4 arms — the C++'s MenuGame.cpp:1012-1013 / :1016-1017, VERIFIED: F3 = `reloadAssets(false)` = the level's assets, F4 = `reloadAssets(true)` = the pack's) but bypassed the handler entirely: no guard, no `reloadAllShaders`, no `setIndex` restore, no result dialog, no `select.ogg`, no `setIgnoreAllInputs(2)` — they called the (now-real, 29i1) seam directly and dropped the result. The C++'s help lines ("F3 - RELOAD LEVEL ASSETS (DEBUG MODE ONLY)" / "F4 - RELOAD PACK ASSETS (DEBUG MODE ONLY)" — MenuGame.cpp:1225-1226; the port's `menu.rs:224-238` already carried the exact strings) were already in place from 13b. (a) **The handler**: `Shared::reload_assets` ports the C++ verbatim — the guard `state == LevelSelection && dialog.empty() && config.debug()` (MenuGame.cpp:3094-3099; `Config::getDebug` = the `official ? default : value` half, Config.cpp:1117-1120 — the port's `MenuConfig::debug()` seam delegates to `oh_config::Config::get_debug`, which carries that exact expression), then `assets.reload_all_shaders()` (:3101), `assets.reload_assets(entire_pack, <level>)` (:3104-3114), `set_index(<active drawer's current index>)` (:3116 — the C++'s `setIndex(lvlDrawer->currentIndex)` "loads the new levelData"), the UPPERCASE result + the close line (:3118-3120 — `ssvu::uppercasify` = the ASCII half; the C++ appends the close line BEFORE uppercasifying, the port uppercasifies then appends — the line is already uppercase, identical result), `play_sound_override("select.ogg")` (:3124), `show_dialog_box` (:3125), `set_ignore_all_inputs(2)` (:3126 — the C++'s :3121-3123 "Needs to be two" comment: the first release is the key that popped the box, the second closes it — the 29b3 counter gate consumes them). (b) **The level-id seam contract**: the handler passes the ACTIVE DRAWER's current entry's FULL asset id (the `level_data_ids[current_index]` — the port's menu-level id) and `RealMenuAssets::reload_assets` resolves it into the C++'s `packId` / `packPath` / `id` triplet from the `Assets` store (the C++'s `levelData->packId/packPath/id` — `levelData` is the `setIndex`-loaded entry). The first cut passed `current_level.id` instead — the `RealMenuAssets::level` seam's `LevelInfo.id` is the BARE id (the C++'s `LevelData::id`) while the FAKE seam's is the full drawer id; the drawer-id form is the seam's contract (every other `MenuAssets` method takes the full asset id), so the handler uses it. (c) **The windowed reentrancy fix**: the handler's `setIgnoreAllInputs(2)` fires INSIDE the menu input manager's `update` (the F3/F4 bind), so the `IgnoreInputsCallback` could not re-borrow the manager's `RefCell` (the C++ has no such lock — the plain `game.ignoreAllInputs`, MenuGame.cpp:3389-3402). The `MenuSlotAdapter` now carries an `ignore_all_pending: Rc<Cell<Option<bool>>>` latch: the callback stashes the flag (a `Cell` write needs no borrow), and the `update_input` applies it to the manager right after the `update` returns. Observationally identical: the manager reads `is_ignoring_all` only at `update` entry (the C++'s `Manager.hpp:31-33` `if(isIgnoringAll) return;` half), so a mid-fire set affects the next `update` in BOTH implementations. The F3/F4 arms rewired to `m.reload_assets(false/true)`. Tests: `oh-menu/tests/reload.rs` (5 — the three guard arms: debug off / a dialog open / the wrong state, each pinning "no reload call, no dialog, no sound, the counter untouched"; F3 = the level reload (the `entire_pack == false` half) + the uppercase dialog + the counter 2; F4 = the pack half) + `oh-app/tests/windowed.rs` (3 — end-to-end over the REAL `_RELEASE` assets: F3 in debug → the dialog's first line `LEVEL DATA APEIROGON.JSON SUCCESSFULLY LOADED` (the cube pack's level: file + style + music data sections, the `nullSoundId` early return ends the output) + the close line + `ignore_inputs == 2`; F4 → `LEVELS SUCCESSFULLY RELOADED` + the counter; the default (non-debug) config's F3 = a no-op — the guard, end-to-end). Gate green: 877 + 8 (29i1) + 5 + 3 = 893.

**29p1** (uservisible-gameplay — the workshop-pack discovery, this commit) — oh-steam WRITES `workshopCache.json` (the C++ `load_workshop_data` cache-write port, Steam.cpp:238-244) but no Rust code READ it, so installed workshop packs vanished exactly in the Steam-down case the cache exists for. The C++'s discovery (Assets.cpp:750-764 + `loadWorkshopPackDatasFromCache`, Assets.cpp:662-710) is now ported: (a) the new `oh_assets::WorkshopPacks` trait — the C++'s `steam_manager*` member's `Assets`-facing surface (ONLY the two methods `loadAllPackDatas` uses: `is_initialized()`, Steam.cpp:973-975, and `workshop_pack_folders()`, the C++'s `for_workshop_pack_folders`, Steam.cpp:931-934) — and the new `Assets::new_with_workshop(root, headless, levels_only, workshop: Option<Arc<dyn WorkshopPacks>>)` constructor (the C++'s `mSteamManager` argument, main.cpp:380/:211); `Assets::new` delegates with `None` (the C++'s `steamManager == nullptr` state — the ~20 existing call sites untouched). `oh-steam` implements the trait for `SteamManager` (the orphan-rule home: the type is oh-steam's, the trait's is oh-assets' — oh-steam gained a direct oh-assets dep, already transitive through oh-game/oh-net). (b) `load_all_pack_datas`'s workshop half, the C++'s precedence mirrored: the local `Packs/` scan runs FIRST; then, iff a manager exists — initialized → the LIVE enumeration (the installed folders, Assets.cpp:752-754 — the cache is NOT consulted: the live list fully replaces it); uninitialized → `load_workshop_pack_datas_from_cache` (Assets.cpp:662-710 verbatim: the `::loadAssets` channel's "Workshop cache file does not exist. No workshop packs to load" / "Loading workshop packs from cache" / "[ERROR]: Cannot locate cache array in workshop cache file" / "Cache array is null. No workshop packs to load" / "Cache array is empty. No workshop packs to load" lines, the null/non-array shared arm, the SUCCESS-ONLY `loadInfo.addFormattedError(cacheError)` quirk — the read error is discarded on the four false arms) → `tryLoadPackFromPath` per cached path. (c) **The `packDatas` first-wins fix** (the task's verified precedence): the C++'s `packDatas.emplace` keeps the FIRST entry on a duplicate packId (the `Packs/` scan first → local packs beat cached/workshop duplicates); the port's `load_pack_data` used `insert` (last-wins) — now `entry().or_insert()` (the unconditional `packInfos.push` stays, matching the C++'s `packInfos.emplace_back`). (d) The C++'s workshop paths (the `GetItemInstallInfo` `folderBuf` / the cache's entries) carry NO trailing slash and the C++'s `loadPackData` concatenates `packPath + "/pack.json"`; the port's `load_pack_data` expects the scan's trailing-slash form — the new `try_load_workshop_pack_from_path` normalizes (appends `/`) for BOTH the live and the cache loops. (e) Cache location: the C++ reads CWD-relative `"workshopCache.json"` (the writer's target); the port's root-scoping reads `root/workshopCache.json` (oh-steam's writer keeps the CWD — the two coincide in production, where the CWD IS the resolved game root). (f) The compositions now pass the manager where the C++ does: `client.rs`'s mainClient (main.cpp:380), `android.rs` (the same C++ line — the no-SDK manager is uninitialized, so the cache fallback is its live half), and `dispatch.rs`'s mainServer (main.cpp:199 + :211 — the C++'s server constructs the manager AND passes it to `HGAssets`; the port shares the ONE manager object with the engine's seam via a clone, replacing the previous SECOND `crate::steam::steam_manager()` construction that would double-initialize the SDK). The port-only `print_lua_docs` / lifecycle-test fixtures stay on `Assets::new` (no C++ analogue). JSON-error class: the cache read goes through `read_file_with_errors` (no throw); a NON-STRING-CONVERTIBLE `cachedPacks` element (an object/array — numbers coerce, matching the JsonCpp fork's `asString`) → the `Assets` ctor's fatal mapping (the C++ aborts in the uncaught `getExtr` type error — the same documented clean-error class as the pack.json mismatch). Tests: `oh-assets/tests/workshop.rs` (6 — BOTH DIRECTIONS: the live enumeration REPLACES the cache (the cache points at a real "stale" pack that must not appear); the cache fallback when Steam is down (the uninitialized manager's fake live folder is never read — no error line; the cache's duplicate of the local pack keeps the LOCAL entry — the first-wins half); `Assets::new` (the nullptr state) never reads an EXISTING cache; the four cache false arms (no file / missing key / null / non-array / empty); a cached path without `pack.json` → the C++'s `Error loading pack data` line, non-fatal; a non-string cache element → the fatal); `oh-steam/tests/workshop_packs_seam.rs` (1 — the seam impl: the initialized fake-SDK manager exposes the installed folders in SDK order; the NoSteam manager is uninitialized + empty). NOT established: the real Steam SDK's install paths (the `GetItemInstallInfo` values with a live Steam client — host CI has no Steam; the seam contract is pinned by the scripted-SDK doubles + the cache fixtures). Gate green: 893 + 6 + 1 = 900.

**Batch hygiene:** gate green after every item (fmt + build + clippy `-D warnings` + workspace tests; 877 → 885 → 893 → 900); one commit per item, each named for its row id (29i1 = `e137d098`, 29b9 = the commit after it, 29p1 = this commit); explicit-path staging throughout (no `git add -A`); `.agents/audits/*.md` untouched; no device work (the phone's ADB listener is down for the whole batch — none of the three items needs a device; the reload dialog's on-device visual is PENDING, host-pinned as above).

## Report — 029misc-batch (29b4, 29u1, 29o1, 29h1, 29a4, 29m1 — one commit each)

**29b4** (uservisible-gameplay, this commit) — the `-p <pack> -l <level>` CLI boot now STARTS the level, closing the last uservisible gap of the 029b menu lane. (a) **The tail**: `Shared::load_command_line_level` (`menu_game.rs`) gained its C++'s last statement — `self.play_selected_level()` (MenuGame.cpp:1778), placed after the `change_state_to(LevelSelection)` tail (the C++ order — the state is set at :1775, `setIndex` at :1773-1774, then `playSelectedLevel()` at :1778). The success path's `playSelectedLevel` fires the `new_game` seam with the boot-selected level (pack id, the drawer's full level id, `firstPlay = true`, the `setIndex`-reset 1.0× `diffMultIdx` mult, `executeLastReplay = false` — the C++ `playSelectedLevel`'s `lvlDrawer` expressions, MenuGame.cpp:2369-2383); the windowed `new_game` callback (windowed.rs:2336-2349) does `game.new_game(...)` + `engine.set_game_state(GameSlot::Game)` — the C++ `fnHGNewGame` (main.cpp:442-449: `hg.newGame(...)` + `window->setGameState(hg.getGame())`). (b) **The failure paths**: `init_command_line_level` / `load_command_line_level` now return the C++'s `bool` result (the early returns at MenuGame.cpp:1701-1765 — invalid pack / no levels / invalid level / no profiles — BEFORE the tail — leave the C++ `gameState` null; the first frame's `assert(isValid())` would fire). The windowed boot's `-p -l` branch (windowed.rs) now captures the result and forces `GameSlot::Menu` ONLY on failure (the documented deviation from the module docs: the C++ failure path's null-state crash is not reproduced — the window points at the menu, the tail's pre-play `LevelSelection`). (c) **The wrong comment**: the in-code justification at the old windowed.rs:2431-2434 (and the module-docs "Boot-dispatch null states" bullet) claimed the C++ `-p -l` path leaves a null game state and crashes — true only of the FAILURE path; the success path sets the state at main.cpp:446-448. Both reworded to the failure-path-only form (the `-p`/`-l` "to immediately run level" doc, main.cpp:96-97,:104-105, re-verified). Tests: `oh-menu/tests/boot.rs` +1 (`load_command_line_level_tail_starts_the_level_via_the_new_game_seam` — the tail fires the `new_game` seam exactly once with the boot-selected level's args; the menu state is the tail's pre-play `LevelSelection`, the C++'s unchanged-menu-state half) — the existing `load_command_line_level_selects_and_lands_in_level_selection` (the `None`-callback menu: the C++ `if(fnHGNewGame)` no-op guard, MenuGame.cpp:2370) now documents the no-op; `oh-app/tests/windowed.rs`: the old `boot_with_p_and_l_lands_in_level_selection_and_play_reaches_the_game_slot` REPLACED by `boot_with_p_and_l_starts_the_level_immediately` (success: `GameSlot::Game` after `boot`, the menu still `LevelSelection` with the level selected, `game.get_level_data().id == "apeirogon"`) + new `boot_with_an_invalid_pack_or_level_lands_in_the_menu` (BOTH failure arms — invalid pack, invalid level — land in `GameSlot::Menu`, no crash, no level started). The menu-state tests that used `-p cube -l apeirogon` as a shortcut to reach `LevelSelection` (enter/down/F3/F4/F3-noop) now take the C++'s NORMAL user flow through a data-root SANDBOX (new `TestSandbox` + `sandboxed_windowed_client` + `boot_to_level_selection`: symlinks the read-only `_RELEASE` subtrees, a writable `Profiles/` with the C++ test's `testProfile`, `boot(None,None,None)` → `advance_boot_screen` ×2 (LoadingScreen → EpilepsyWarning → `playLocally`'s `SLPSelectBoot`) → `ok_action` (the profile item's `exec` → SMain) → `ok_action` ("LEVEL SELECT" → LevelSelection), then `change_pack_to` + `set_index` steer to the canonical cube/apeirogon — the plain boot lands on the FIRST SELECTABLE pack's first level (the tutorial's `babysteps` — the ctor's `init_boot_level` `setIndex(0)`, NOT the pack-priority order), so the steer restores the old shortcut's selection); the game-slot tests (rotation/screenshot/force-restart/pause) now use the new `-p -l` boot directly (the `ok_action` line dropped — the boot's tail fires `fnHGNewGame`). NOT established: the on-device `-p -l` boot (the phone's ADB listener is down for this batch — no device work, no temporary `debuggable` flag; the host tests pin the seam contract end-to-end over the real `_RELEASE` assets, the device confirmation is PENDING). Gate green: 900 + 2 = 902.

**29u1** (internal, this commit) — `setMouseCursorVisible` is wired end-to-end; the `Window::set_mouse_cursor_visible` flag (oh-render `window.rs`) was a flag-only store never called from any menu/game path. The C++ cursor logic is entirely menu-state (all four handlers are on the `MenuGame` ctor's `game.onEvent` bus — `GameEngine::handleEvent` dispatches to the ACTIVE state only, GameEngine.hpp:58-62 / GameState.hpp:90-93; the game state never re-shows, so a started level's hidden cursor persists). Ported 1:1: (a) **the seam**: new `MenuWindow::set_mouse_cursor_visible` (the C++ `MenuGame::setMouseCursorVisible` → `window.setMouseCursorVisible(x)`, MenuGame.cpp:3641-3644); the `MenuWindowSource` (oh-app) backs it with a shared `Rc<Cell<bool>>` cursor flag (the C++ `MenuGame::mouseCursorVisible`, MenuGame.cpp:197) created `true` in `build` (the C++ ctor's `setMouseCursorVisible(true)`, :245); the `FakeWindow` records it. (b) **the menu call sites**: `play_selected_level` hides (MenuGame.cpp:2373 — inside the `if(fnHGNewGame)` guard, the port's `let Some(cb)` — the cursor hides only when a `new_game` seam is wired, exactly the C++); `handle_mouse_wheel` re-shows (MenuGame.cpp:304-306 — the C++'s `hasFocus` gate is the documented S1 no-menu-mouse-state gap; the handler is only reached in the menu state). (c) **the frame loop's other two handlers**: the C++'s `KeyPressed` → hide (:278) and `MouseMoved` → show (:295) are observed PER FRAME in `tick_fields` (the menu-state event feed's once-per-frame approximation, the documented 025c/025f note), menu-state only: a key newly pressed this frame (the `InputState` bitset vs the previous frame's) hides; a changed raw mouse pixel re-shows (new `Window::mouse_pixel()` accessor — the C++ `actuallyMoved` delta test, :284-296; the first frame's `None` previous is NOT a move). The hide-then-show order makes a same-frame key+move resolve to "show" (the mouse presence is the cursor's intent). The frame's final value syncs to the window's own `mouse_cursor_visible` flag after the update burst (the C++ applies it in `setMouseCursorVisible`; the port keeps the window's field the observable state). Tests: `oh-menu/tests/cursor.rs` (3 — `play_selected_level` hides over a recording `FakeWindow`; the unwired-`new_game` guard leaves it visible; `handle_mouse_wheel` re-shows after a hide) + `oh-app/tests/windowed.rs` (1 — `cursor_hides_on_keypress_and_shows_on_mouse_move_in_the_menu` over the REAL frame loop: a key press hides the window's flag, a release does NOT re-show (the C++ has no release cursor handler), a mouse move re-shows — the window's `mouse_cursor_visible` pinned). NOT established: the actual OS cursor hide/show (the headless window records the flag only — the real `sf::RenderWindow::setMouseCursorVisible` GL/winit call is the live-window half, a manual check; the flag's toggling at the C++'s four call sites is pinned host-side). Gate green: 902 + 4 = 906.

## 029misc-remainder — deviations rows (29o1, 29a4, 29m1)

**29o1** (the harvested commit `52a470d8` added no deviations row; added here) — the C++ `std::signal` REPLACES the prior SIGINT handler (one active at a time: the headless default at `main.cpp:627-634` is installed BEFORE dispatch and the windowed handler REPLACES it at `:340-350`, the server's at `HexagonServer.cpp:1389-1390`). `signal-hook` CHAINS (every registered action fires, in registration order), so a "default + replacement" would fire the headless `exit(1)` FIRST and never the windowed/server handler. The port therefore installs PER MODE (`oh-app/sigint.rs` `install_headless` / `install_server`; `windowed.rs::install_sigint` for windowed) — the active handler per mode is identical to the C++. Safe-port (the C++'s own `TODO (P2): UB`): the handler body is an async-signal-safe `write(2)` of a pre-built line + either a raw `_exit(1)` (headless) or one atomic store (the server's `globalRunning = false`; the C++'s `globalListener.close()` deferred to the run loop's exit). NOT established: the end-to-end signal delivery (SIGINT → handler → the printed line + exit-1 / stop-flag) is a manual check — the handler BODY (the `headless_line` / `server_line` text + the exit/store) is pinned in-process, but launching the binary to send a real signal is the racy blocking check this lane exists to avoid.

**29a4** — the death/replay log lines were already landed by 029a1 (`14167fc6`); no code change. `Attempting to send and save replay...` is in BOTH `death` (HexagonGame.cpp:980) and `save_replay` (HexagonGame.cpp:555), and the `Successfully saved new compressed replay file '...'` success line (HexagonGame.cpp:1093-1094) + the failure line are in `RealReplayStore::save` (oh-app/replay_store.rs). The M8–M12 surface gaps (game.md) are all NOT-NEEDED in the port's structure (no dead API added for symmetry): **M8** `refreshTrigger` (HexagonGame.cpp:461-464) — the C++ delegates to the SSV input manager; the port wires the rebind at the composition (the windowed `trigger_refresh_cb` re-points BOTH input managers via `Manager::refresh_triggers`, input_manager.rs:399, cited to HexagonGame.cpp:467-470) — the engine has no method (the port's `HexagonGame` does not wrap an SSV `GameState` member). **M9** `getGame()` (HexagonGame.cpp:1527) — the `window->setGameState(hg.getGame())` handoff (main.cpp:448/502) is the composition's `GameEngine::set_game_state(GameSlot::Game)` (game_state.rs). **M10** `getColor(int)` (HexagonGame.cpp:1537) — the only C++ caller is `drawText`'s `getColor(0)` (HGGraphics.cpp:732), inlined in `bridge.rs`'s `draw_frame` (`style.get_color(0)` + the B&W/empty → BLACK gate). **M11** pack accessors — `getPackId`/`getPackName` ported (`get_pack_id`/`get_pack_name`); `getPackData` = the `SharedState.pack_data` clone (the registry is immutable after load); `getPackDisambiguator` / `getPackAuthor` / `getPackVersion` have NO C++ call sites (not ported). **M12** `getAssets()` (HexagonGame.cpp:1611) — NO C++ call sites (not ported).

**29m1** — the replay string bytes + the bounded file read; two deviations:
**(a) the non-UTF-8 name's lossy display/filename** — the C++ `player_name` / `pack_id` / `level_id` are `std::string` (raw bytes, NO UTF-8 requirement). The port now holds them in `oh_replay::ByteString` (raw `Vec<u8>`), so a legacy C++ `.ohr` with a non-UTF-8 name round-trips LOSSLESSLY — both the file form and the packet form (`serialize_to_packet` / `deserialize_from_packet` route through the file-form `serialize` / `deserialize`; `Reader::read_str` fills the `ByteString` with the raw bytes, NO UTF-8 check — the C++ `std::string`). The ONE remaining deviation: the C++ writes those raw bytes to `create_filename`'s path and to the display (`std::cout << _player_name`); the port's `String`-based `Display` / `create_filename` (via `ByteString::to_string_lossy`) is the lossy UTF-8 form (U+FFFD for invalid bytes) — Rust `String` is UTF-8-only. The wire bytes round-trip losslessly; only the human-facing display/filename is lossy. (The old module-docs UTF-8 deviation #1 — "deserializing a non-UTF-8 name marks the result failed" — is RESOLVED.)
**(b) the bounded file read** — the C++ `deserialize_from_file` reads into the 2 MiB static buffer (Replay.cpp:363-370): a larger file is a short read → failbit → false (fails, does not OOM). The port previously did `std::fs::read` (the WHOLE file) before the cap check, so a hostile multi-GB file OOMed. Now the port opens the file and reads at most `BUF_SIZE + 1` bytes (`File::take(BUF_SIZE + 1)` → `read_to_end`), failing past the cap WITHOUT reading the whole file. For a >2 MiB file the C++'s `deserialize(buf, full_size)` reads PAST the 2 MiB buffer (a latent out-of-bounds read, UB); the port's clean "fails, does not OOM" is the port-only safety fix (behaviour identical for files ≤ 2 MiB). `CompressedReplayFile::deserialize_from_file` is unchanged (the C++ reads the COMPRESSED file unbounded; the 2 MiB cap is the DECOMPRESSION destination, not the compressed file).

## Report — 029-tail batch (the last four 29 rows: 29v1, 29g1, 29w1, 29q1 — one commit each)

**29v1** (internal, commit `49c23991`) — the 29s1 bug class is now mechanically uncatchable. NEW `crates/oh-lua/src/snippets.rs` = the single source of truth for every executable embedded Lua string: `REDEFINE_IO_OPEN` (LuaScripting.cpp:144-147, byte-verified), `REDEFINE_RANDOM` (LuaScripting.cpp:159-168, byte-verified), `OVERRIDE_SCORE_VALIDATION` (HGScripting.cpp:635-638, byte-verified; the `{variable}` splice point = the C++'s `mVar`); `sandbox.rs` / `level_control_level.rs` now use the constants. The sweep — every `executeCode` call site in the C++ tree: those three ARE the complete executable-embedded set; the rest is user input (the console's `u_log(<cmd>)` / bare `<cmd>`, HGUpdate.cpp:1518/1522) or file contents (`runLuaCode` / `runLuaFile`, HGUpdate.cpp:1595, Utils.cpp:29/:95). `destroyMaliciousFunctions` (LuaScripting.cpp:178-219) is NOT a snippet — a fixed `clearVariable` sequence; the port mirrors it 1:1 incl. the double `package.loadlib` clear (:207/:217) — verified. Divergence found + fixed: the `l_overrideScore` splice carried 9 tabs where the C++ carries 8 (byte-identical now; Lua-insensitive, but the verbatim contract is the point). No second 29s1-class bug found. The guard: `snippets.rs`'s table-driven `every_embedded_snippet_compiles` — every row runs through the C++ `executeCode` path (load + pcall, chunk name `"chunk"`) and must not raise `LuaError::SyntaxError`; a snippet without a row is exactly how 29s1 slipped through. Negative half `the_29s1_extra_paren_splice_does_not_compile` pins the 29s1 bug as a `SyntaxError` (the guard has teeth). (mlua 0.10.5 exposes no LuaJIT compile-only handle — `Chunk::compile` is Luau-only — so the guard asserts on the `execute_code` result; for these snippets the C++ itself executes them at init/call time, so a clean `Ok` is the C++-faithful assertion.) Citations in the touched files re-derived (sandbox.rs module docs 156-175 → 156-176, 178-218 → 178-219; `redefine_random` doc 160-169 → 159-168). NOT established: nothing host-relevant.

**29g1** (internal, commit `5607a122`) — the Lua error surface, harvested from the failed lane's WIP + one repair (the WIP's missing `use crate::add_lua_fn_try;` import in `shaders.rs` broke the build — the macro IS defined + `#[macro_export]`ed at `context.rs:596`; adding the import makes the crate green: 38 passed, 0 failed). The three items: (1) `shdr_getDependencyShaderId` now propagates the validation failure (the `add_lua_fn_try!` match arm: `log_dependency_lookup_failure` + `return Err`) instead of silently returning `size_t(-1)` — the C++ aborts the running script and logs "Fatal error while looking for Lua dependency" (Utils.cpp:116-160/:177-207 → LuaScripting.cpp:1267-1311; the sibling `u_execDependencyScript`'s propagation was already faithful, only this path deviated); (2) the two missing diagnostic lines — the `u_execDependencyScript` failure path (the new `Err` arm in `exec_script.rs`, the C++ line before the rethrow equivalent) and the `redefineIoOpen` / `redefineRandom` catch-block lines (LuaScripting.cpp:150-153 / :171-174 — the port logs them at the single production call site, `api/mod.rs`, where the `ssvu::lo` sink (the `GameApi` backend) is in reach, before the `Err` propagates; the caller's `.expect("init_lua_api failed")` is the C++'s uncaught-exception abort, so the diagnostic is the added half); (3) the three `cw_setVertexColor*` setters now share the C++'s mod-256 channel wrap (`rem_euclid(256)` — `sf::Color`'s `int -> int16_t -> uint8_t` narrowing, LuaScripting.cpp:326/:374/:405: 300 → 44, 256 → 0, -1 → 255) instead of the port's internally inconsistent pair (the single setter's `try_from(...).unwrap_or(255)` + the helper's `clamp(0, 255)`). NOT established: no NEW behaviour tests in this batch — the three changes are pinned by the re-derived citations + the 38 pre-existing `oh-lua` tests; the harness is in place for a future `ssvu::lo`-line pin.

**29w1** (uservisible-cosmetic, commit `f6ca5ad3`) — the credits-bar textures are the real `Assets/` PNGs, the menu's half of the 29e1 class: `MenuDraw::new(assets_dir, ...)` loads `creditsBar1.png` + the three `CREDITS_BAR_IDS` frames through `bridge::load_texture_or_null` (made `pub(crate)` — the C++ ctor's `assets.getTexture`, MenuGame.cpp:170-171; a missing file → the 1×1 `nullTexture` fallback) and applies the C++ `initAssets` `setSmooth(true)` (MenuGame.cpp:821-828). `draw()` sets the `creditsBar2` frame every frame from the id it borrows from the menu state BEFORE the `refresh_if_stale` (the C++ sets it in `update()`, before `draw`'s `refreshCamera`, whose `getLocalWidth(creditsBar2)` reads the current texture — MenuGame.cpp:3157; the cycle itself is MenuGame.cpp:2848-2850, the ids at MenuGame.hpp:135-137 = `CREDITS_BAR_IDS` — order mirrored, verified against the C++). **29q1** (internal, commit `4b9b2ee0`) — the citation/comment drift sweep (D-014: verified citations).
